## Review

**7 out of 10, Effective.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear route: agenda, strategic update, performance, business update, market context, then position-by-position pages, and the recurring layouts (colored claim banner, chart with dated event callouts, average-cost line) make the long run of holdings easy to follow. Many pages state their conclusion in a banner headline and back it with a readable chart or table, such as slides 8, 21, 47 and 54. Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck. Slide 1 is bare, and the 85 pages end with team profiles rather than a close.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the banner-headline-plus-chart layout with dated event callouts and the dashed average cost line on slides 47, 53 and 59, and the consistent contributor and detractor tables on slides 29 and 30.

**Most useful repair:** Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

**What would change the score:** I would raise the 7 to an 8 if the chart pages were decluttered and the strategic update pages carried visuals, and I would lower it if slides beyond the 72 contact sheet pages are as dense and unstructured.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 85 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck is the annual investor presentation of Pershing Square Holdings, Ltd. (PSH), dated February 11, 2026, and covers its 2025 results. It opens with a title slide and a disclaimer on performance, risk and the limits of forward-looking statements. The agenda then moves through a chairman's introduction, a board overview, a strategic update covering debt financing, the discount to NAV, the PSUS listing and the HHH transaction, followed by a 2025 performance review with annual and cumulative return tables and contributor and detractor breakdowns. Next come a business and organizational update, a market developments section on valuation and earnings growth since COVID, and an equity portfolio update covering holdings such as Brookfield, Uber, Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac, Amazon and Universal Music Group. The deck closes with an organizational update and team profiles. Slides worth studying include the contributor and detractor slides, which use a consistent layout for gross attribution, and the position-by-position slides, which pair a share price chart with dated event markers and a stated average cost.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Pershing Square Holdings, Ltd. annual investor presentation have?**

The deck has 85 pages. Slide 3 lists the agenda, which moves through the chairman's introduction, strategic update, 2025 performance review, business update, market developments, equity portfolio update and Q&A.

**What does Pershing Square Holdings, Ltd. say about its long-term debt financing strategy?**

Slide 9 states that PSH debt is covered 5.1x by total assets as of 12/31/25, with laddered maturities from 2027 to 2039. It also gives a weighted-average maturity of 6 years and a weighted-average cost of capital of 3.6%.

**What were the 2025 returns for Pershing Square Holdings, Ltd.?**

Slide 8 says that in 2025 PSH's NAV grew 20.9% and total shareholder return was 33.9%. Slide 21 shows the same 2025 figures alongside the returns of the S&P 500, FTSE 100, MSCI World and HFRX indices.

**What does Pershing Square Holdings, Ltd. say about HHH's acquisition of Vantage Group Holdings?**

Slide 16 says that in December 2025 HHH signed a definitive agreement to acquire Vantage Group Holdings, Ltd. for $2.1 billion in cash, including up to a $1 billion investment from PSH. The slide states the acquisition is expected to close in Q2 2026, subject to regulatory approvals.

**Layout**

Title top-left above a thick navy rule spanning the content width. Below it, a three-column table (photo, name/role, bullets) with a blue header row and six light-blue rows of differing heights. A full-width indigo banner with white bold text sits under the table. Page number bottom right. Margins about 4% left and right.

**Visuals**

Six headshot photographs in the left column, one per row, each about 5-6% wide and 8-9% tall, unframed. 1) Rupert Morley: smiling man with grey swept-back hair, dark suit, plain background. 2) Charlotte Denton: woman with long wavy brown hair, light top, soft light background. 3) Andrew Henton: man with glasses and receding hair, dark suit and tie with pinkish accent, grey background. 4) Bilge Ogut: woman with light brown hair pulled back, dark jacket, neutral background. 5) Jean-Baptiste Wautier: dark-haired bearded man in a grey cardigan, seated in front of a red patterned chair. 6) Halit Coussin: woman with long dark hair, dark jacket, black background. Thin grey horizontal separator lines between rows. Small dash bullets before each experience line. A thick navy horizontal rule under the title.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in blue #3A75C4 Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height. Table header is a #3A75C4 bar with white Arial Black text, about 1.8%. Row cells are filled pale blue #E7F9FF with grey separator lines. Director names are bold black Arial at about 1.8%. Roles and bullets are regular black Arial at about 1.5%. Bullets are small dashes. The bottom banner is indigo #333399 with white bold Arial at about 3%. Navy #000080 rule under the title. Square corners, no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

- Review: Many pages state their conclusion in a banner headline and back it with a readable chart or table, such as slides 8, 21, 47 and 54.

### Slide 9

**Text**

Long-Term Debt Financing Strategy
PSH’s ability to access low-cost, investment-grade, long-term debt is an
important competitive advantage for PSH
PSH has attractive credit characteristics
Simple, transparent, and highly liquid investment portfolio
Significant asset coverage: PSH debt is covered 5.1x by total assets(1)
Underlying portfolio companies are diversified across industries and have relatively
low commodity exposure and cyclical risk
Proven investment strategy with a long-term track record
Facilitates opportunistic issuances
Laddered maturities from 2027 to 2039 are well matched with PSH’s
long-term investment horizon
Weighted-average maturity of 6 years; weighted-average cost of capital of 3.6%
No margin leverage
No mark-to-market covenants
(1) Total Assets is defined as Net Asset Value plus Total Debt as of 12/31/25.

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in heavy Arial Black, blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. Banner fill #4D83CB with white bold Arial text about 3.3% height. **Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule, followed by a full-width green banner with a two-line white message. Below is a bold underlined chart heading, then a line chart spanning the slide with callout boxes placed over the plot and linked by dotted grey leader lines. Legend is centred below the x-axis, footnotes sit bottom-left and the page number is bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of Total Debt as % of Total Capital, monthly, Jun-15 to Dec-25. Y-axis 12.0% to 24.0% in 2.0% steps, with a vertical axis line and tick marks. X-axis labels every six months from Jun-15 to Dec-25, rotated 45 degrees, with tick marks. No gridlines. One dark slate-blue (#44546A) line of about 3 px width. The series starts at about 12.9% in Jun-15, dips to about 12.2%, rises to about 20.5% in mid-2016, and stays between about 17.5% and 20.7% through 2019. It peaks at about 21.1% in mid-2019, falls to about 15.3% in early 2019/20 and about 16.2% in mid-2020, and is about 19.5% in Dec-2020. It peaks at about 23% in Oct-2021 and at about 24% in mid-2022, then declines to about 14.5% in early 2025, jumps to about 19.4%, dips to about 16.9%, and ends at 19.5% (footnote 1) in Dec-25. Legend: short dark slate line with 'Total Debt as % of Total Capital'. Callouts: 9 yellow boxes (#FEF1B0) with thin grey-brown outline for issuances and private placements, 2 blue boxes (#B0C8E8) for redemptions, 2 green boxes (#B6D7C1) with dark green outline for rating upgrades. Dotted grey leader lines (about 3 px) connect each box to its point on the line. No logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in Arial Black, bold blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. Navy #000080 horizontal rule under the title. Green banner #509467 with white bold Arial text, about 3.3% height. Chart heading bold black Arial, underlined. Callout text in Arial Narrow Bold, black, about 2% height, centred. The 19.5% label is bold Arial #44546A. Footnotes are italic Arial, about 1.1% height. Square corners and no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 11

**Text**

Strategies to Address the Discount
Strong investment performance
Compound annual NAV net returns of 22.6% for the last eight years (the “Permanent Capital Era”)(1)
Corporate actions
Gained premium listing on the London Stock Exchange in May 2017, when PSH ranked 139th in the FTSE by market capitalization
Initiated quarterly dividend in Q1 2019. Revised dividend policy in Q2 2022 whereby dividends now increase with NAV
Quarterly dividend is now $0.1837 per share, an 84% increase from $0.10 per share in 2019
PSH has repurchased 74 million shares, representing 29.7% of initial shares outstanding, at an average price of $25 and average discount to NAV of 29%(2)
In 2025, PSH repurchased 6.6 million shares at an average price of $56 and average discount to NAV of 29%
PSCM affiliates have accumulated a 28% ownership stake through outright purchases(3)
Global marketing efforts outside of the United States
Inclusion of PSH in the FTSE 100 index in December 2020. Now the 58th largest company by market capitalization(4)
AIC reclassification from Hedge Funds to U.S. Equity
Retained Cadarn Capital for increased UK/European/Asian distribution and LodeRock Advisors for increased Canadian distribution
The board continues to believe that the most powerful driver of long-term shareholder returns will be continued strong absolute and relative NAV performance
Note: Pershing Square began its ‘‘permanent capital era’’ on 1/1/18 by focusing on growing its permanent capital base through generating and compounding long-term returns and renewing its commitment to its core investment principles.
Past performance is not necessarily indicative of future results. All investments involve the possibility of profit and the risk of loss, including the loss of principal. Please see the additional disclaimers and notes to performance results at the end of this presentation.
(1) The 1-year, 5-year, and 10-year compound annual NAV net returns for PSH were 20.9%, 14.1%, and 15.5%, respectively. See slide 8 for further details.
(2) Initial shares outstanding reflect public and management shares outstanding as of 4/30/17. Repurchase data as of 2/9/26.
(3) Ownership percentage based on current shares outstanding. Includes Pershing Square employees and affiliates and charitable entities associated with Pershing Square employees or Pershing Square affiliates.
(4) FTSE 100 companies ranked by market capitalization as of 2/9/26.

### Slide 13

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

**Text**

PSUS IPO Update
Made the decision to re-evaluate PSUS’s IPO structure to address investor feedback
Received substantial interest from retail and institutional investors over PSUS’s initial two-month marketing phase in July 2024
However, investor considerations around potential aftermarket trading dynamics prompted us to re-evaluate the transaction structure
Launch of PSUS should result in material reduction of PSH’s performance fees
As part of PSH’s amended Fee Offset Arrangement, PSH’s performance fees will be reduced by 20% of the management fees earned on PSUS
PSH’s net performance fee percentage will be lower in years with lower returns
13

**Layout**

Left-aligned title near the top with a full-width dark navy rule beneath it. Below, two bold first-level bullets, each followed by two indented second-level bullets, in a single column with wide left and right margins. Page number sits at the bottom-right corner. Lower third of the slide is empty.

**Visuals**

A thick dark navy horizontal rule spans from about x 4% to x 96% under the title at y about 15%. Two blue right-pointing triangle bullet markers in front of the first-level bullets. Four small blue filled square bullet markers in front of the second-level bullets.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background. Title in bold heavy blue (#3A75C4) Arial Black style sans-serif, about 4.4% of slide height. First-level bullets in bold black Arial, about 2.8% of slide height. Second-level bullets in regular black Arial, about 2.4% of slide height. Accents (triangle and square markers) blue #3A75C4. Title rule dark navy #000080, about 0.4% thick, no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 14

**Text**

HHH Strategic Transaction with Pershing Square
In May 2025, Pershing Square invested $900 million of primary capital as
part of a plan to transform HHH into a diversified holding company
The Pershing Square management company (Pershing Square HoldCo, L.P.) purchased 9
million newly issued HHH shares for $100 per share
Along with PSH’s and the private funds’ interest, Pershing Square’s beneficial ownership of HHH
has increased to 47%, with voting power capped at 40%
Investment by Pershing Square enables HHH to begin its transformation into a diversified
holding company seeking controlling stakes in high-quality, durable growth companies
HHH signed a definitive agreement to acquire Vantage Group Holdings, Ltd. in December 2025
Existing HHC real estate subsidiary will continue its core MPC business with no change in strategy
Bill Ackman rejoined HHH’s Board as Executive Chairman and Ryan Israel joined the
Board and HHH’s executive team as Chief Investment Officer
The entire Pershing Square team will support HHH in its transformation in exchange for
management fees
14

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width navy rule; a full-width blue banner with two lines of white text beneath; below it a bulleted list indented from the left with bold first-level bullets and regular second-level bullets, left-aligned. Page number in the bottom right corner.

**Visuals**

A thick navy horizontal rule under the title spanning the content width. A solid blue rectangular banner holds the headline statement. First-level bullets are small blue right-pointing triangles; second-level bullets are small blue squares.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold heavy sans-serif (Arial Black) in medium blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. The banner is filled #3A75C4 with white bold Arial text at about 3.3%. First-level bullets are bold black Arial at about 2.6%; second-level are regular black Arial at about 2.4%. The rule is navy #0A0A8C, about 0.6% thick. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 15

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

**Text**

Benefits to PSH from HHH Strategic Transaction
Reduction in PSH management fees
PSH management fees will be reduced dollar for dollar by the proportionate share of fees
paid to PSCM by HHH attributable to the HHH common stock held by PSH(1)
Improved HHH should enhance the value of PSH’s stake in the company
Leverages Pershing Square’s leadership and investment expertise
Asymmetric hedging strategy can mitigate HHH’s exposure to macroeconomic risks
Differentiated ability to source and execute private transactions
Improved access to equity and debt capital markets
Transaction should widen investor interest in HHH driven by expanded investment strategy
and Pershing Square’s broad retail and institutional following
PSH and HHH will pursue distinct yet synergistic investment mandates
Note: This slide contains Pershing Square’s current views and expectations and is provided for illustrative purposes only. This slide does not constitute a guarantee or promise with respect to any outcome and there can be no assurance that
these expectations will be achieved.
(1) PSH currently owns 16.5 million shares of HHH.
15

### Slide 16

**Text**

HHH’s Acquisition of Vantage
In December 2025, HHH signed a definitive agreement to acquire Vantage
Group Holdings, Ltd., a leading specialty insurance and reinsurance company
Purchase
Price
$2.1 billion in cash, including up to a $1 billion investment from PSH
HHH to retain Vantage’s book value accretion from signing to close (“locked box”)
Purchase price represents 1.5x estimated year-end 2025 book value and is
expected to represent a ~1.4x P / BV multiple at close
Timing
Expected to close in Q2 2026, subject to regulatory approvals
Funding
Sources
$1.2 billion in HHH balance sheet cash
Up to a $1 billion backstop minority investment from PSH
Investment will take the form of non-interest-bearing preferred stock issued by HHH(1)
PSH’s preferred stock will be exchangeable for the common stock of Vantage if not
redeemed by HHH by the end of the seventh fiscal year post-transaction
Based on the highly cash-generative nature of HHH’s real estate assets, we expect that
HHH will redeem PSH’s preferred stock and increase its economic ownership of Vantage
to 100% within three years
(1) The preferred stock will bear a mandatory dividend in the event of a default on HHH’s obligation to repurchase the stock in certain circumstances.
16

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule. Below it a full-width medium-blue banner with a two-line white headline. Three rows follow, each with a dark navy label box at left (Purchase Price, Timing, Funding Sources) and bullets to the right, separated by thin dashed grey horizontal lines. Footnote at bottom left, page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Solid dark navy label boxes with white bold text. Blue right-pointing triangle bullets for main points and small blue square bullets for sub-points. Two thin dashed grey horizontal divider lines. A thick dark navy horizontal rule under the title. A blue banner rectangle. No charts or photos.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in blue #3A75C4 Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height. Banner fill #3A75C4 with white bold Arial text about 3.2%. Label boxes #1D3B62 with white bold Arial about 2.6%. Main bullets black bold Arial about 2.4%, sub-bullets regular Arial about 2.2%. Blue accents #3A75C4. Rule navy #0A0A8C. Flat style, square corners, no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 17

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

**Text**

Vantage Acquisition Strategic Rationale
We believe the acquisition of Vantage is an ideal transaction to begin
HHH’s transformation into a diversified holding company
Well-diversified insurance platform across lines of business with both specialty
insurance and reinsurance exposure
Highly experienced management team
Limited risk to existing reserves (Vantage was launched in 2020)
In-place regulatory licenses and credit ratings (A.M. Best and S&P A- Rating)
Leverages Pershing Square’s investment expertise
Addition of a higher-return, faster-growth insurance subsidiary materially
accelerates HHH’s overall growth profile and diversifies its earnings stream
Provides additional opportunities for reinvesting HHH’s real estate subsidiary's
excess cash, which will initially be used to redeem PSH’s preferred stock

### Slide 18

**Text**

PSH Preferred Investment Terms
PSH / Investment
Investment from PSH will take the form of non-interest-bearing preferred / stock issued by HHH(1)
Up to $1 billion, split into fourteen equally-sized tranches
PSH’s preferred stock will be exchangeable for the common stock of Vantage / if not redeemed by HHH by the end of the seventh fiscal year post-closing
Repurchase / Call Option
At the end of each fiscal year, for the first seven years post-transaction, HHH / will have the right to repurchase one or more tranches of the preferred stock
Repurchase price will be cash equal to 1.50x the then book value of Vantage / multiplied by the preferred tranche(s)’ ownership percentage(2)
Governance / Terms
PSH’s preferred stock will generally be pari passu with HHH common stock; no / liquidation preference
Subject to a mandatory repurchase by HHH in the event of, among others, a / change of control of HHH or Vantage
Preferred investment approved by independent directors of both PSH and HHH
Over time, we expect HHH to fully exercise its call options on all tranches of PSH’s preferred / stock and increase its economic ownership of Vantage to 100%
(1) The preferred stock will bear a mandatory dividend in the event of a default on HHH’s obligation to repurchase the stock in certain circumstances.
(2) On an as exchanged basis. Subject to a minimum repurchase price equal to the original issue price plus 4% per annum through the date of repurchase.

### Slide 19

**Text**

PSH Preferred Investment Highlights
We believe PSH’s preferred investment is highly compelling both on its
own merits and in the significant value creation it will catalyze for HHH
Highly attractive return profile on a stand-alone basis
Contractual exit liquidity mechanism at a pre-determined multiple(1)
Economically similar to an investment in the PSH portfolio
Prospective returns on PSH’s preferred investment should reflect growth in Vantage’s book value
plus the benefit of P / BV multiple expansion from entry to exit
Growth in Vantage’s book value should closely track overall PSH portfolio returns (subject to
insurance regulatory requirements)
Vantage transaction materially enhances the value of HHH, a core holding of PSH
Vantage transaction will accelerate HHH’s transformation into a diversified holding company,
enhancing its growth and return profile
PSH’s existing investment in HHH common stock is ~40% larger than its maximum preferred
commitment
(1) Subject to a minimum repurchase price equal to the original issue price plus 4% per annum through the date of repurchase.
19

**Layout**

White slide with a left-aligned bold blue title near the top and a navy rule beneath it. A full-width blue banner with a two-line white statement sits below. Beneath it, a left-aligned checklist of four bold main points with green check marks in the left margin, and indented blue square-bulleted sub-points under points 3 and 4. **Layout**

**Visuals**

Line chart of cumulative net returns, monthly points from '04 to '26 on the x-axis (labelled yearly) and a y-axis from (250%) to 2,750% in 250% steps with tick marks. Series 1, a thick navy line (PSLP / PSH net return), rises slowly from 0% in '04 to about 750% in early '15, drops to about 390% in '16, stays about 400-450% through '17, falls to about 390% at '18 start, then climbs to about 2,700% in mid-'25 and ends at about 2,503% in '26. It has a dip to about 1,250% in '22. Series 2, a thick gray line (S&P 500), rises gradually from 0% to about 220% in '18, then about 854% at the end, with a dip to about 370% in '22. A vertical gray dashed line sits at '18, and a gray arrow points from the text box to this line. Three curly brackets under the x-axis group '04 to mid-'15, '15 to '17 and '18 to '26, each with a center tick pointing down to its label. The white text box has a thin black border, a blue title with a dark blue underline, a dark red banner with white bold text, green check marks and small blue square bullets. The stats box has a navy thin border. Series labels are in colour at the right of the lines. No logos.

### Slide 23

**Text**

Commitment to Core Principles
Pershing Square’s Core Investment Principles
Key Business Characteristics
Simple, predictable, free-cash-flow-generative business
Formidable barriers to entry
Limited exposure to extrinsic factors that we cannot control
Generally, strong financial profile
Minimal capital markets dependency
Typically, North American-listed, large-capitalization companies
Attractive Valuation
Fair price “as is”, but a substantial discount to optimized value
Base case valuation not reliant on future M&A or platform value
Exceptional Management and Governance
In-place or identified management with high degree of integrity, track record of success, and appropriate incentives
Typically, no controlling shareholder
Occasional Mispriced Probabilistic Investments
Must offer highly asymmetric return to compensate for possibility of permanent loss of capital
Expected to represent a small percentage of fund capital in the aggregate and an appropriate “return on invested brain damage”
Pershing Square
Simple, predictable, free-cash-flow-generative business
Formidable barriers to entry
Limited exposure to extrinsic factors that we cannot control
Strong financial profile
Attractive valuation
Minimal capital markets dependency
Large market capitalization
Exceptional management & governance
Note: The above is intended to be illustrative. While PSCM generally seeks investments with some or all the above characteristics, there is no guarantee that PSCM will be able to identify investments or create a portfolio that reflects these characteristics.
23

**Layout**

Title at top left with a dark blue rule beneath spanning the width. Left column holds a grey textured panel with the bulleted principles in four headed sections and a blue Pershing Square logo banner at its bottom. Right side holds a 2x4 grid of light grey tiles, each with a navy icon above a centered caption. Footnote runs along the bottom, with the page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Left: a grey speckled, torn-edge paper-textured panel (about 42% wide) with a thin dark blue underline below the panel heading and small blue right-pointing triangle bullets. Below it is a solid blue banner with the white Pershing Square logo (a white square-S monogram plus the white two-line wordmark 'Pershing Square'). Right: eight light grey tiles in 2 columns x 4 rows, each with a dark navy (#0B3A66) flat icon: stack of dollar bills with a $ sign; brick wall; circular arrows around a gear; document with a money bag with $; balance scale; line chart with rising arrow on axes; two office buildings; group of three business people.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in heavy Arial Black blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. A thick navy #000080 rule under the title. Section headings in Arial Black about 1.5% height in colours: dark blue #333399, maroon #800000, blue #3A75C4, green #509467. Body bullets in bold Arial black about 1.4% height. Tile captions in regular Arial black about 2% height. Tiles fill #F2F2F2 with no outline or shadow. Banner fill about #00578D. Footnote in small italic Arial.

26

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule. Beneath it a wide yellow banner holds the chart heading, then two light-blue header boxes (PSH on the left, PSLP / PSH on the right) over a clustered column chart split by a vertical dashed line. A rounded legend box is centred below the chart. A small italic note runs along the bottom, with the page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Clustered column chart with no axis title and no y-axis shown, only a light grey baseline. Six categories, each with a blue PSLP / PSH bar and a green S&P 500 bar, and a bold black value label above each bar. Values: 1-Year 21% vs 18%; 5-Year 93% vs 96%; 8-Year 410% vs 192%; 10-Year 323% vs 298%; Since PSH Inception (12/31/2012) 418% vs 507%; Since Firm Inception (1/1/2004) 2,644% vs 836%. The Since Firm Inception bars are by far the tallest, with the blue bar reaching about 2,644% at the top of the plot. A vertical grey dashed line separates the first five categories from the last one. The legend sits in a white rounded rectangle with a thick navy border and has a blue square marker for PSLP / PSH and a green square marker for S&P 500. A yellow rectangle with a thin grey border holds the heading. Two light-blue rectangles with thin navy borders hold the PSH and PSLP / PSH headers.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in heavy Arial Black style sans-serif, medium blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height, with a thick navy #000080 rule beneath. The banner is pale yellow #FEF1B0 with a grey outline. Heading text is bold Arial: 'Net Returns' in blue #2C5893, 'vs.' in black, 'S&P 500' in green #3C6F4D, and the subtitle is bold italic black. Chart bars are blue #4472C4 and green #509467. Labels are bold Arial black at about 2% of height. The header boxes are #E8EFF8 with navy outlines. The footnote is small italic Arial in black. Sharp corners except the legend box, which has a rounded border of about 2% radius. No shadows or gradients.

### Slide 33

**Text**

Key Developments in the Last Twelve Months
Strong portfolio gains and progress on strategic initiatives drove a PSH
total shareholder return of 34% in 2025, 1.9x the return of the S&P 500(1)
New investments in Hertz, Amazon, and Meta
Exited investments in Chipotle, CPKC, and Nike in 2025, as well as Hilton
in 2026
Began transformation of HHH into a diversified holding company via a
$900mm strategic transaction with PSCM
HHH signed a definitive agreement to acquire Vantage for $2.1bn, which will
be partially funded by up to $1bn of preferred stock issued by HHH to PSH
Continued focus on redesigning the PSUS transaction structure
(1) PSH return represents the USD-based total shareholder return of PSHD with dividends reinvested.
33

**Layout**

Title top-left, left-aligned, with a thick navy rule beneath spanning the content width. Below it is a full-width green banner with a two-line white headline. Five check-marked bullets are stacked vertically with generous spacing, text indented from the check marks. Footnote bottom-left, page number bottom-right.

**Visuals**

A thick navy horizontal rule under the title (about #000080). A solid green rectangular banner (#509467) with no rounded corners holding white bold text. Five green check-mark glyphs (Wingdings ✓, #509467), one at the left of each bullet.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold blue #3A75C4, Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height. Banner text white bold Arial about 3.3% of height. Bullet body text black bold Arial about 3.1% of height. Accents are green #509467 and navy #000080. Footnote in small black italic Arial about 1.1% of height. Page number in small Helvetica. No shadows or gradients.

Footnote bottom-left and page number bottom-right.

Banners use white bold Arial text at about 3.3% height: blue #4D83CB, green #509467, grey #7F7F7F, dark red #800000. Table header text is white bold Arial at 2.2%; body values are black bold Arial at 2.2%. Footnote is black italic Arial at 1.1%. Flat shapes with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 35

**Text**

Robust New Idea Generation
We completed the acquisitions of two core investments and one
opportunistic investment over the last twelve months
amazon
(13% of capital)(1)
Largest cloud business by market share, with secular IT infrastructure
and process tailwinds, and the dominant retail e-commerce operator
Meta
(10% of capital)(1)
Leader in the fast-growing digital advertising space and one of the
clearest beneficiaries of AI integration
Hertz
(2% of capital)(1)
Turnaround opportunity driven by improved industry dynamics,
rationalized fleet composition, and operational enhancements
Macro-driven market volatility, rapidly changing investor narratives, and idiosyncratic,
misunderstood situations create opportunities for Pershing Square
Note: This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
(1) As of 12/31/25.

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule, followed by a full-width green banner. A pale yellow callout band spans the width near the bottom, with footnotes at bottom left and page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Three company logos: Amazon wordmark in dark navy-black lowercase with an orange curved smile arrow beneath; Meta logo with blue infinity-loop symbol and dark grey 'Meta' wordmark; Hertz logo in black bold italic with a yellow bar underneath. Three blue right-pointing triangle bullets. Navy thick rule under the title. Two grey horizontal divider lines between rows. Green and pale yellow filled rectangles.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold blue #3A75C4 Arial Black. Navy #000080 rule. Green banner #509467 with white bold Arial text. Body text black Arial regular. Captions black italic Arial. Yellow callout #FEF1B0 with black bold italic Arial text. Blue triangle bullets #3A75C4. Grey dividers #808080. Flat style, no shadows or rounded corners.

**Layout**

One flat light-blue rectangle (#99CCFF) with no outline, shadow or gradient, behind the title. No images, charts or icons.

### Slide 40

**Text**

Stock Market Performance Since COVID Has / Been Strong
The S&P 500 has increased 112% cumulatively, or 13% per annum, from / 2020 to 2025
S&P 500 Index:
7,500
6,500
5,500
4,500
3,500
2,500
1,500
3,231
6,846
Dec-19
Dec-20
Dec-21
Dec-22
Dec-23
Dec-24
Dec-25
Source: Bloomberg. Excludes dividends.
40

**Layout**

Two-line left-aligned title at top-left with a thick navy rule beneath it spanning the content width. A full-width blue banner with a white two-line headline sits below, then an underlined chart label at left and a large line chart filling the lower two-thirds. Source note at bottom-left, page number at bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'S&P 500 Index:'. Y-axis from 1,500 to 7,500 with ticks every 1,000 at 1,500, 2,500, 3,500, 4,500, 5,500, 6,500, 7,500 (bold black labels). X-axis is time with ticks at Dec-19, Dec-20, Dec-21, Dec-22, Dec-23, Dec-24, Dec-25. A single slate-dark-blue-grey line (#3F4A5E, thick about 3pt) tracks the S&P 500 daily. It starts at 3,231 (labelled) in Dec-19, drops to about 2,300 in early 2020, rises to about 4,800 by Dec-21, falls to about 3,600 in late 2022, climbs to about 6,100 by Dec-24, dips to about 5,000 in spring 2025, and ends at 6,846 (labelled) in Dec-25. Only the start and end values are labelled. No legend, no gridlines. Black axis lines with tick marks on the left and bottom axes.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in heavy Arial Black, mid-blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. Thick navy #000080 rule under the title. Banner fill #4D83CB with white bold Arial text about 3.3% of slide height. Chart label bold Arial black underlined at 2.2%. Axis labels bold Arial black 1.8%. Source note italic Arial 1.1%. Flat design with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart titled 'Annual S&P 500 Index Price Change:' with six categories 2020-2025 on a horizontal black axis with tick marks. There is no value axis. Dark slate-blue series 'Change in NTM EPS' and mid-blue series 'Change in P/E Multiple'. Values in white inside segments: 2020: P/E +23% (above axis), EPS (5%) (below axis). 2021: EPS 34% (above), P/E (5%) (below). 2022: EPS 3% (above, small), P/E (22%) (below). 2023: EPS 6%, P/E 17% stacked above. 2024: EPS 13%, P/E 9% stacked above. 2025: EPS 14%, P/E 2% stacked above. Total labels in cream boxes with thin black borders: 2020 16%, 2021 27%, 2022 (19%) below the bar, 2023 24%, 2024 23%, 2025 16%. Legend with small square markers: dark slate 'Change in NTM EPS', mid-blue 'Change in P/E Multiple'. No logos or icons.

**Visuals**

Two column charts with dark slate-blue bars (#44546A), no gridlines or value axes, a thin black baseline with tick marks, and bold category labels beneath. **Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width navy rule, followed by a green banner with a two-line white headline. Below is an underlined subheading, a weight table at left, a clustered bar chart filling the right and centre, and a pale yellow callout box across the bottom. Source line at bottom left, page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Left: a boxed table (black border) with header 'Weight' and ten company rows alternating grey (#BFBFBF) and white, each with a company logo and weight: NVIDIA 7.5%, Apple 6.5%, Alphabet 6.4%, Microsoft 5.0%, amazon 3.6%, Meta 2.8%, BROADCOM 2.6%, Tesla 2.5%, Berkshire Hathaway Inc. 1.7%, Lilly 1.6%. Final row 'All Top 10' 40.3% on pale yellow. Black dotted ovals circle the Alphabet, amazon and Meta rows. Right: clustered column chart with no y-axis, a black baseline, and four categories each with two bars. Series 2026E is dark slate (#44546A) and 2027E is blue (#4472C4). Values: Top 10 25% and 20%; Next 490 7% and 11%; S&P 500 14% and 14%; S&P 500 Equal Weight 8% and 12%. Bold black data labels sit above the bars. The legend is centred below the category labels with square markers. The bar heights are proportional to the values.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. The title is bright blue #3A75C4 in Arial Black. A thick navy #000080 rule sits under the title. The green banner #509467 carries white Arial bold text. The subheading is black bold underlined Arial. The bottom callout is pale yellow #FEF1B0 with black bold italic Arial. Chart labels are bold Arial black. The source text is small italic Arial. There are no shadows or rounded corners.

**Layout**

**Visuals**

Two bar charts, each with two bars and a black baseline, no axes or gridlines, no legend. Right chart, NTM Consensus P/E Multiple: bars in medium blue, '14-'19 Avg. = 17x, Current = 22x; value labels above bars. Grey header bars above each chart. A thick navy horizontal rule under the title.

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold blue #3A75C4, Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height. Green banner #509467 with white bold Arial text about 3.2% of height. Grey header bars #7F7F7F with white bold Arial text about 2.6%. Yellow callouts #FEF1B0 with black bold Arial about 1.8%. Left bars #44546A, right bars #4472C4. Rule is navy #00008B, about 0.5% thick. Body labels bold black Arial. Source in small italic Arial at about 1.1%. Flat design with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 47

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A line chart of forward P/E over five years is annotated with dated narrative events, showing how sentiment shifts line up with the valuation point at which the position was bought.
- Review: Many pages state their conclusion in a banner headline and back it with a readable chart or table, such as slides 8, 21, 47 and 54.
- Worth keeping: Keep the banner-headline-plus-chart layout with dated event callouts and the dashed average cost line on slides 47, 53 and 59, and the consistent contributor and detractor tables on slides 29 and 30.

**Layout**

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'NTM P/E Multiples – Last Five Years'. Y axis 'NTM P/E Multiple' from 12.0x to 30.0x with ticks every 3.0x (30.0x, 27.0x, 24.0x, 21.0x, 18.0x, 15.0x, 12.0x). X axis dates 2/9/21 to 2/9/26 at six-month ticks. Two series: GOOG in medium blue and S&P 500 in dark charcoal. GOOG starts about 27x in early 2021, peaks about 28x in mid-2021, falls to about 14x in late 2022, recovers to about 21x in 2023–24, dips to about 14.5x in spring 2025, then surges to about 27.7x by the end of 2025. S&P 500 starts about 22.3x, falls to about 16x in late 2022, then rises to about 22–23x and ends about 22x. A horizontal grey dashed line at about 15.9x runs across the chart. Dotted grey leader lines link the callouts to the chart points. Legend at the bottom centre with a blue line for GOOG and a black line for S&P 500. Boxed table with a thick black border overlaid on the chart: columns GOOG, S&P 500, GOOG vs. S&P 500; rows Current 26.7x, 21.8x, 1.2x; 3/2/2023 15.7x, 17.7x, 0.9x; 5-Year Avg. 20.3x, 20.1x, 1.0x; 10-Year Avg. 20.5x, 19.0x, 1.1x. Dual-axis line chart. Left axis is NTM P/E Multiple, 15.0x to 50.0x in 5.0x steps. Right axis is NTM EPS, $1.00 to $4.00, labelled at $1.00, $1.50, $2.00, $2.50, $3.00, $3.50, $4.00. The dark grey/black NTM P/E line starts at about 45-48x in July 2024, falls to about 36-40x, peaks at about 42x in Oct 2024, drops to about 31x in Nov 2024, hovers at about 25-31x through 2025, and ends at about 21x. The blue NTM EPS line rises from about $1.55 in July 2024 in steps to about $2.4 (Nov 2024 jump), about $2.65 and then a jump to about $3.1 in Mar 2025, climbs to about $3.35 by Oct 2025, jumps to about $3.8 in Nov 2025, and ends at about $3.56. A grey dashed horizontal line sits at about 26.6x (about $2.00 on the right axis). A grey-bordered light grey label box reads 'NTM P/E At Inception (1/7/2025): 26.6x' at lower left of the plot. Brookfield Corporation
Leading alternative asset manager with high-quality and rapidly growing
cash flows trading at a significant discount to intrinsic value
Leading asset-rich alternative asset manager with deep domain expertise
Owns 73% of $84bn publicly-listed asset manager (Brookfield Asset Management) with
significant value derived from asset-light, recurring management fee streams
We believe that deep domain expertise and best-in-class returns position Brookfield to
benefit from multi-trillion-dollar wave of AI-related infrastructure investment
Financial profile poised to inflect in the near-term
Double-digit earnings growth in 2025 below structural potential as carried interest still
below target and Brookfield invests for growth
Near-term outlook supports acceleration to ~25%+ earnings growth
Growth supported by a step-function increase in carried interest realization and the rapid
scaling of BN’s Wealth Solutions business
Attractive long-term financial outlook
Management targeting nearly $7 of earnings-per-share in 2030, or ~25% compounded
BN’s valuation of ~16x earnings remains a discount to our estimate of intrinsic value
and comparable US-peers
Source: Company filings. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.

**Visuals**

Brookfield Corporation wordmark logo in dark navy serif type, stacked on two lines, at upper left of the left column. **Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule. Below it a full-width mid-blue banner holds a two-line white headline. An underlined bold chart heading sits left, then a large line chart fills the lower two-thirds with three yellow callout boxes. Footnotes in small italics run across the bottom; page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of BN share price, 4/11/2024 to 2/9/2026. Y axis 'Share price' (underlined, rotated), $25 to $50 in $5 steps, with tick marks. X axis labels every ~2 months: Apr-24, Jun-24, Sep-24, Nov-24, Feb-25, Apr-25, Jun-25, Sep-25, Nov-25, Feb-26, black axis lines with ticks. A single blue (#3A5FC0) jagged line, about 3px thick, starts at about $26.5 in Apr-24, rises to about $30 by May-24, dips to about $27 in Jun-24, climbs to about $35 by Sep-24, about $41 in Nov-24, about $38-41 in Jan/Feb-25, dips to about $30 in Apr-25, rebounds to about $45 by Jul-25, about $48.5 in Sep-25, peaks about $49 in Dec-25 and ends at about $47 (labelled $47 in blue at right). A grey dashed horizontal line at about $28 spans the plot width, labelled by a light grey box 'Average Cost at Announcement: $28*'. Three pale yellow callout boxes with grey borders connected by grey dotted lines: 5/2/2024 box with a vertical dotted line down to the line start near Apr/May-24; 9/10/2025 box with a dotted line to the Sep-25 peak; 7/31/2025 box with a dotted line up to the Jul-25 area of the line.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in Arial Black, blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. Dark navy #000080 thick rule under the title. Banner fill #4D83CB with white bold Arial headline about 3.3% height. Chart heading black bold Arial underlined 2.2%. Axis labels grey #595959 Arial 2.1%. Callouts #FEF1B0 fill with grey border, bold Arial Narrow black text 2.0%. Average cost label box #F2F2F2 with grey #7F7F7F bold text and border. Footnotes italic Arial 1.1% black. Square corners, no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 54

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Three-column structure pairs a financial outlook with industry developments and a valuation note, giving a compact template for presenting a single holding's thesis.
- Review: Many pages state their conclusion in a banner headline and back it with a readable chart or table, such as slides 8, 21, 47 and 54.

**Text**

Uber Technologies (“UBER”)
Uber
High-quality business positioned for continued rapid earnings growth / trading at a compelling valuation on misplaced industry concerns
Near term results are exceptionally strong and accelerating
Bookings increased 19% in 2025 with accelerating momentum; new all-time highs / on users, engagement, and trip growth
Pre-tax operating profit grew 50% with strong operating leverage
Financial outlook remains extremely attractive, and valuation is compelling
Medium-term outlook positions UBER for continued teens-plus bookings growth and / 30%+ growth in earnings-per-share
High rate of growth incorporates planned autonomous vehicle (AV) investments
Newly issued non-GAAP earnings disclosure highlights how inexpensive UBER / remains at only 21x NTM earnings
Industry developments consistent with our “partnership” investment thesis
UBER targeting commercial AV operations in 10+ cities by the end of 2026
UBER x Waymo driving best-in-class asset utilization; future expansion likely
Nvidia’s recently launched open-source Alpamayo AV model accelerates development / lead-times and broadens access to AV technology
Tesla FSD still under development; potential for future win-win partnerships remain
Source: Company filings. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
54

**Layout**

Title top-left above a full-width navy rule. Below, a narrow left column holds the Uber logo, separated by a thin vertical black line from a wide right column of three stacked coloured header bars, each followed by check-mark bullets. Source footnote at bottom-left, page number at bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Black 'Uber' wordmark logo at left in the upper part of the content area. A thick navy horizontal rule under the title spans nearly the full width. A thin black vertical line separates the logo column from the content column. Three coloured header bars: indigo, mid-blue and maroon, plus a green one. Green check-mark glyphs precede each main bullet. A small blue square marks the single sub-bullet.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold Arial Black style sans-serif, blue #3A75C4, about 4.4% of slide height. Header bars have white bold Arial text, 2.8-3.0% of height: indigo #333399, blue #3A75C4, maroon #800000, green #509467. Body bullets in bold Arial black #000000, about 2.4-2.6% of height. Sub-bullet in regular Arial. Check marks green #509467 (Wingdings 2). Navy rule #000080. Flat rectangles, no shadows, no rounded corners. Footnote in small italic Arial.

### Slide 55

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

**Text**

**Layout**

Title at upper left above a navy rule spanning the content width. A full-width blue banner with a two-line white headline sits below. An underlined chart heading is at left, with a large line chart filling the rest of the slide. Yellow callout boxes are placed over the chart. A small italic footnote block is at the bottom, with the page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of UBER share price, 1/7/2025 to 2/9/2026. The Y axis is Share price, $60 to $105 in $5 steps. The X axis has ticks at Jan-25, Mar-25, Jun-25, Sep-25, Nov-25 and Feb-26. A thick blue line starts at about $66 in Jan-25, dips to about $64.5 in early Feb, and rises to about $78 in mid-Feb. It falls to about $64.5 in early Apr, rises to about $97 in late June, and fluctuates around $90-$100 through Sep-Oct with a peak of about $100. It drops to about $83 in mid-Nov, rebounds to about $92.5, falls to about $79 in Dec, recovers to about $87 in early Jan, and ends at about $74 (labelled $74 in blue to the right of the line end). A grey dashed horizontal line sits at about $67 (average cost). A grey-bordered light grey label reads 'Average Cost at Announcement: $67*'. Eight pale yellow callout boxes with grey borders connect to points on the line via grey dotted leader lines. The chart has black left and bottom axes, with tick marks on the x axis.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. The title is bold blue #3A75C4 in Arial Black. The banner is #4D83CB with white bold Arial text. The navy rule is #000080. Chart axis labels are grey #595959 Arial. Callouts are #FEF1B0 with black Arial Narrow Bold text and grey borders. The data line is blue #4472C4, about 3 px thick. The dashed line and dotted leaders are grey #7F7F7F. The footnote is small black italic Arial.

### Slide 56

**Text**

Fannie Mae (“FNMA”) and Freddie Mac (“FMCC”)
FannieMae
Freddie Mac
Fannie and Freddie shares more than tripled in 2025 as the Trump
administration reiterated its commitment to an eventual privatization
The administration has repeatedly emphasized three key objectives
Enhance home affordability by compressing the spread of mortgages over Treasuries
Demonstrate a near-term mark to market for the taxpayers’ ownership in the GSEs
Maximize long-term value of the taxpayers’ interest in Fannie and Freddie
We believe the solution we published in November achieves all three goals
Three simple steps can immediately be taken by Treasury and FHFA
Account for the repayment of Treasury’s Senior Preferred Stock
Exercise Treasury’s warrants to purchase 79.9% of the common stock of the GSEs
Re-list Fannie and Freddie on the New York Stock Exchange
Conservatorship would continue until the administration can carefully execute an exit
No disruption to the mortgage and MBS markets, GSE operations, degree of government control and oversight, or GSE recapitalization through earnings retention
We believe YTD share price declines greatly underestimate how quickly President Trump can act to
drive a re-rating of shares and unveil a taxpayer asset worth ~$300 billion
Source: Company filings. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
56

**Visuals**

Two logos at top left: Fannie Mae (green wordmark in serif type with a small green emblem) and Freddie Mac (blue and green house-shaped icon with black serif text 'Freddie Mac'). Thin vertical black line right of the logos. Blue triangle bullets and small blue square sub-bullets. Navy horizontal rule under the title.

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in blue #3A75C4 Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height, with a navy #000080 rule beneath. Header bars: indigo #333399, mid blue #3A75C4, dark red #800000, all with white bold Arial text at about 2.9-3.0% of slide height. Body text black Arial; first-level bullets bold, sub-bullets regular, about 2.4% of slide height. Accents blue #3A75C4 for bullets. Bottom callout pale yellow #FEF1B0 with black bold italic Arial at about 2.5%. Footer source in small italic Arial about 1.1%. Flat shapes, square corners, no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 57

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.

57

**Layout**

Title at top left above a dark navy rule spanning the width. A full-width blue banner with a two-line white headline sits below. Beneath it a bold underlined chart subtitle on the left, then a large line chart filling the slide with yellow callout boxes scattered above the lines and dotted leader lines pointing to the data. Right column holds end-value labels and a grey box. Legend centered under the chart, italic footnotes across the bottom, page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of FNMA (blue) and FMCC (red) share prices from Oct-13 to about Feb-26. Y axis 'Share price' from $0 to $16 in $2 steps; x axis Oct-13 to Oct-25 yearly ticks. Both lines start about $1.5 in Oct-13, spike to about $5.8 in mid-2014, hover about $2-4.5 through 2016-2019, peak about $4 in late 2019, fall to about $0.4-1 in 2021-2023 (low around Oct-22/23), rise to about $1.5 in late 2024, then surge to about $15.3 (FNMA) and about $14.1 (FMCC) in Sept 2025, ending at $8.24 (FNMA, blue) and $7.23 (FMCC, red). Two grey dashed horizontal lines at about $2.9 and $2.45 (average cost FNMA/FMCC) run across the chart, with a pair of dashed lines at about $3.0 and $2.4 on the right. Legend: blue line FNMA, red line FMCC. Eleven pale yellow callout boxes with thin grey border and bold condensed black text, each linked by a grey dotted leader line to a point on the lines. Grey light box (#F2F2F2, grey border) at right with average cost text. Navy horizontal rule under the title.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold wide Arial Black-style sans-serif, medium blue #3A75C4, about 3.8% of slide height. Navy #000080 rule under the title. Banner fill #4D83CB with white bold Arial text about 3.3%. Chart subtitle black bold Arial, underlined. Callouts #FEF1B0 fill with thin grey outline, bold Arial Narrow black text about 2%. Axis labels grey #595959 Arial. Blue #4D83CB and red #C0504D value labels bold. Grey box text #7F7F7F bold. Footnotes italic Arial about 1.1% black. No shadows or gradients, square corners.

### Slide 58

**Text**

Amazon (“AMZN”)
Amidst broader market volatility in April 2025, we were able to / opportunistically initiate a position in Amazon at a highly attractive valuation
Amazon operates two of the world’s great, category-defining franchises
AWS is the leading cloud hyperscaler in a highly concentrated market
Amazon.com is the largest global e-commerce retailer, enabled by a one-of-a-kind / logistics network that fulfills over $700 billion in gross merchandise value annually
Both businesses supported by decades-long secular growth trends, occupy dominant / market positions, and leverage scale to continually improve their value proposition
AI adoption extends and potentially accelerates AWS’s growth runway
Despite an impressive 20% growth rate in 2025 at a $140 billion run-rate revenue scale, / AWS’s ability to meet customer demand was limited by capacity constraints
We expect the company’s planned doubling of datacenter capacity through 2027 to be / rapidly absorbed by compute demand from scaling AI inference workloads
Significant margin expansion opportunity in Amazon’s retail business
Structural potential to nearly double retail profitability, driven by increasing mix of / higher-margin advertising revenue, network density, and automation initiatives
Broad merchandising mix has limited cost impact from tariffs on results to-date
Source: Company filings. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
58

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule. Amazon logo sits in a narrow left column, separated by a thin vertical black line from a right-hand content area with four full-width coloured header bars, each followed by check-mark bullets (two of the bars have bullets beneath them: blue, green, red; the navy bar is a lead-in banner). Source footnote bottom left, page number bottom right.

**Visuals**

Amazon logo at upper left: black lowercase 'amazon' wordmark with an orange curved smile arrow beneath. Thin dark navy horizontal rule under the title. Thin black vertical line at left of the content column. Seven small green check marks as bullet icons. Four solid filled rectangles as header bars: indigo, mid blue, green, maroon.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold blue #3A75C4 Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height. Header bars in white bold Arial at about 2.8% height: indigo #333399, blue #3A75C4, green #509467, maroon #800000. Bullet text in black bold Arial at about 2.4% height with green #509467 check marks. Navy rule #000080. Footnote in small black italic Arial. Flat style with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

59

**Layout**

Title at top-left above a navy rule spanning the content width. Full-width blue banner with two-line white headline below it. Underlined chart heading at left, then a line chart filling the lower two-thirds with three yellow callout boxes and a grey label box. Italic footnotes in small type across the bottom, page number at bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of AMZN share price from 4/21/2025 to 2/9/2026. Y axis 'Share price' (bold, underlined, rotated) runs $150 to $270 with ticks every $20 ($150, $170, $190, $210, $230, $250, $270). X axis has ticks labelled Apr-25, Jul-25, Sep-25, Nov-25, Feb-26. The thick blue (#3A75C4) line starts at about $167 in late April 2025, jumps to about $189, reaches about $211 in May, and climbs to about $230 by August. It hovers between $212 and $238 through September, dips to about $213 in mid-October, and rises to about $230 by late October. It peaks at about $254 in early November, then ranges $222-$249 through December and January. It peaks at about $249 in late January, then drops sharply in early February to end at $209. The end value '$209' is labelled in bold blue at the right. A grey dashed horizontal line sits at $178 across the full chart width, with a grey-bordered light grey box reading 'Average Cost at Announcement: $178*' beneath it. Three yellow callout boxes (#FEF1B0, thin grey border) are connected by grey dotted lines to points on the chart: April 2025 box (top-left, line to about $180 at the start), 10/30/25 box (lower centre-right, line to the late-October point at about $230), 2/5/26 box (top-right, line to the early-February drop at about $223). Axes are black lines with tick marks.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in heavy blue Arial Black (#3A75C4), about 4.4% of slide height, with a navy (#000080) thick rule beneath. Blue banner (#4D83CB) with white bold Arial headline about 3.3% of height. Chart heading in black bold Arial, underlined, 2.2%. Callout text in black bold Arial Narrow, 2.04%, centered. ### Slide 60

**Text**

Universal Music Group (“UMG”)
UMG is a high-quality, capital-light, rapidly growing royalty on greater music consumption
Growth set to accelerate this year
“Streaming 2.0” deals, which incorporate wholesale price increases, should lead to higher subscription revenue growth
New partners and product tiers should allow for better customer segmentation
AI can be a further tailwind to growth
UMG is beginning to monetize AI by partnering with new DSPs creating tools for music discovery and creation
AI will lower costs of creating music and allow UMG to operate more efficiently
UMG trades at a record low valuation
Overhang created by uncertainty around Bolloré’s potential stake sale and U.S. listing delay
Trades at 18x earnings, its lowest valuation ever, and 17x earnings excluding ownership stake in Spotify
Given its market position and decades-long runway for sustained earnings growth, we believe UMG’s current valuation represents a very substantial discount to its intrinsic value
Source: Company filings. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
60

A full-width pale yellow callout sits near the bottom, with the source line at the bottom left and the page number at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

Universal Music Group logo at upper left: black text 'UNIVERSAL' with a globe graphic above it and small letter-spaced 'UNIVERSAL MUSIC GROUP' beneath. Colored rectangles: indigo, blue, green, dark red, and a pale yellow callout box.

61

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width dark navy rule. Below it a full-width blue banner with a two-line white headline. An underlined chart subtitle sits under the banner, then a large line chart fills the slide with seven yellow callout boxes placed around the line. Small italic footnotes run across the bottom margin and the page number sits at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of UMG share price in EUR from Sep-21 to Feb-26. Y axis 'Share price' runs € 15 to € 29+ with ticks every € 2 (15, 17, 19, 21, 23, 25, 27, 29). X axis labels: Sep-21, Apr-22, Oct-22, May-23, Nov-23, Jun-24, Dec-24, Jul-25, Feb-26. One blue jagged daily-price line. It starts at about €25, peaks at about €27.7 in late 2021, falls to about €18 by Mar-22 and to about €17.2 in Oct-22, then rises to about €24 in mid-22. It dips to about €18.5 in spring 2023, climbs to a peak of about €29.4 around Jun-24, drops sharply to about €21 in Jul-24, recovers to about €28.9 in Jan-25, peaks at about €28.3 in Jul-25, and falls to a final value of about €19.73, labelled in blue. A grey dashed horizontal line at about €18.27 spans the plot width, labelled 'Average Negotiated Purchase Price: €18.27*' in a light grey box. Seven pale yellow callout boxes with thin grey borders and black bold narrow text are linked to points on the line by grey dotted leader lines. The y axis has tick marks and a black axis line, and the x axis is a black line with tick marks. There are no gridlines and no legend.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold Arial Black blue #3A75C4. A navy #00007F rule sits under the title. The banner is blue #4D83CB with white bold Arial text. The chart subtitle is black bold Arial and underlined. The chart line is blue #4472C4, about 3 px thick. The axis labels are grey #595959 Arial. The callouts are filled #FEF1B0 with grey #A6A6A6 borders, no rounded corners, no shadow. The callout text is Arial Narrow Bold, black. The final value label is bold blue #3A75C4. Footnotes are tiny black italic Arial. The title is about 4.4% of slide height and the banner text about 3.3%.

**Text**

Alphabet (“GOOG”)
Alphabet
Alphabet is leveraging its core strengths in data, distribution, infrastructure, / and research expertise to successfully execute on its vast AI potential
AI is having a transformative impact on Search & YouTube
AI Overviews & AI Mode demonstrate rapidly improving product velocity in Search
Reaching more than 2 billion users in more than 200 countries, AI Overviews in Search is / effectively the world’s most widely used AI product by a significant margin
Has led to accelerating query growth, with the effect most pronounced amongst younger users
Recently announced Gemini-Apple partnership to power Siri validates AI leadership
Focus on multi-modal AI capabilities (image and video generation) has driven material / improvements to YouTube’s recommendation algorithms and content creation tools
Unique full-stack approach to AI has increasingly differentiated Google Cloud
Cost-advantaged technical infrastructure and world-class models have propelled / Google Cloud into a $70 billion run-rate revenue business, which grew 48% in Q4’25
Rapid improvement in segment profitability with increasing scale
Favorable resolution to recent antitrust review

### Slide 63

**Text**

GOOG Share Price Performance Since Inception
GOOG’s share price including dividend reinvestment increased 65% in
2025 and has increased 3% year-to-date in 2026(1)
GOOG Share Price Performance from 3/2/2023 to 2/9/2026:
11/18/25: Launch of Gemini 3, Google’s most advanced foundational AI model. As a sign of the company’s improved product velocity, Gemini 3 was introduced into Search and Google’s other apps simultaneously with its release
5/11/23: Company holds its annual I/O developer conference, unveiling its new state-of-the-art large-language models and showcasing a variety of ground-breaking AI use-cases
10/29/24: On her first earnings call, newly appointed CFO, Anat Ashkenazi, emphasizes her commitment to “build on the company’s [efficiency] efforts” and “evaluate where we might be able to accelerate work”
9/2/25: Favorable remedy resolution in the DOJ’s case on the company’s default search agreements with Apple and Android OEMs

Note: The performance of GOOG’s share price is provided for illustrative purposes only and is not an indication of past or future returns of the Pershing Square funds. The graph above shows only GOOG’s share price over time, not the value of PSH’s investment in GOOG, and is provided as context for Pershing Square’s views of GOOG. For a variety of potential reasons, such as timing of purchases and sales, use of derivative instruments, and others, PSH’s return on its investment may differ substantially from GOOG’s change in share price. PSH gross return was 77% and net return was 58% from 3/2/23 to 2/9/26.
*Average cost at announcement date based on Pershing Square’s ownership of GOOG (Class C) and GOOGL (Class A). Please see the additional disclaimers and notes to performance results at the end of the presentation.

63

**Layout**

Title at top left above a thick navy rule spanning the content width. Below it is a full-width blue banner with a two-line white headline. Underlined chart subtitle sits at the left. A line chart fills the lower body, with four pale-yellow callout boxes laid over it. Footnotes run along the bottom in small italics, and the page number sits at the bottom right.

**Visuals**

Line chart of the GOOG share price from Mar-23 to Feb-26. Y axis is labelled 'Share price', runs $80 to $380 with ticks every $50 ($80, $130, $180, $230, $280, $330, $380). X axis has date ticks Mar-23, Jun-23, Oct-23, Jan-24, May-24, Aug-24, Dec-24, Mar-25, Jul-25, Oct-25, Feb-26. A single thick blue line starts at about $93 in Mar-23 and climbs to about $125 by mid-2023 and about $190 by mid-2024. It dips to about $145 in Aug-24, peaks near $205 in early 2025, and falls to about $145 in Apr-25. It then rises steeply to about $345 in Jan-26 and ends at $324, labelled in blue at the right end. A grey dashed horizontal line sits at about $94, spanning the full chart width, with a light-grey label box 'Average Cost at Announcement: $94*'. Four yellow callout boxes with grey borders are connected to points on the line by grey dotted lines: 5/11/23 at about $110, 10/29/24 at about $165, 9/2/25 at about $210, and 11/18/25 at about $300. There is no legend. The y axis line and x axis line are black, and x ticks are marked.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in heavy blue Arial Black (#3A75C4) with a navy (#000080) rule beneath. Banner is medium blue (#4D83CB) with bold white Arial text. Callouts are pale yellow (#FEF1B0) with thin grey borders and bold black Arial Narrow text, centred. Chart line is blue (#3A75C4), about 3pt. Dotted connectors and the dashed average line are grey (#7F7F7F). Axis labels are grey (#595959) Arial. Footnotes are italic Arial in black. Square corners, no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 64

**Text**

Meta (“META”)
Ticker: / META
Stock Price: / $677
Market Cap: / $1.7tn
Dominant leader in the secularly fast-growing digital advertising space
Entrenched global user base of more than 3.5 billion daily active users
Daily active user base has continued to grow at a 7% rate in Q4 2025
$200 billion in 2025 revenue, reflecting a 22% annual growth rate
Experienced leadership team led by founder and CEO, Mark Zuckerberg
Operates in two segments: Core Family of Apps and Reality Labs
Family of Apps: Instagram, Facebook, WhatsApp; advertising-based business model
Reality Labs: Wearables and metaverse / augmented reality initiatives; segment is currently loss-making with operating losses representing ~25% of Meta’s overall profit
Currently trading at 22x NTM P/E due to concerns around magnitude of spending plans on AI initiatives
Excluding Reality Lab losses, which represent discretionary growth investments, Meta’s core advertising business is valued at less than 18x NTM P/E
We believe Meta’s current share price underappreciates the company’s long-term upside potential / from AI and represents a deeply discounted valuation for one of the world’s greatest businesses
Source: Company filings and Pershing Square estimates. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.

### Slide 65

**Text**

META Investment Thesis
High-quality advertising business model with increasing returns to scale
Scaling engagement across Meta’s vast user base improves utility for both users and advertisers
Granular visibility into consumer behavior and interests enables highly precise ad targeting, making Meta an essential platform for businesses seeking to maximize their return on ad spend
Meta’s business model is one of the clearest beneficiaries of AI integration
AI-driven content recommendation systems materially enhance user engagement, especially as content has increasingly broadened from the social graph to a wider range of interest-based content
AI leverages Meta's rich first-party data to deliver more relevant and personalized ads
Campaign automation and AI copy creation tools facilitate self-serve model for advertisers
Potential to unlock new use cases and engagement modes (Business AI digital assistants, wearables)
Well-positioned for long-term earnings growth after planned spending ramp in 2026
Significant upside potential from AI supports front-loading infrastructure and talent investments
Historical cost discipline (2023 “Year of Efficiency”) and recently announced budget cuts in the Reality Labs segment highlight focus on balancing AI investments with operational efficiencies
Overbuilding risk mitigated by the core business’s ability to grow into and absorb excess capacity
Strong balance sheet and high-margin core business provide significant financial flexibility
Source: Company filings and Pershing Square estimates. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
65

X axis has ticks and labels Nov-25, Dec-25, Feb-26. Blue line (about 3px thick) starts at about $613, rises to about $636 and $648, then about $674 in mid-Nov, fluctuates between about $644 and $668 through Nov and Dec, and falls to about $604 in late Jan. It then jumps to about $672, peaks at about $738 on 1/28, drops to about $717, about $707, about $669 and about $662, and ends at about $679 labelled $677 in bold blue at the right. A horizontal grey dashed line at about $623 spans the plot width, with a light grey box labelled Average Cost at Announcement: $625* below it at the left. A pale yellow callout box with a grey border sits at top centre-right, connected by a grey dotted line running down and right to the peak at the 1/28 spike. The y axis title Share price is rotated vertical and underlined. No legend.

### Slide 67

**Text**

Restaurant Brands International (“QSR”)
QSR is a high-quality business with significant long-term growth / potential that we believe trades at a highly discounted valuation
Largest businesses outperforming peers
Tim Hortons comparable sales of 3% are outperforming the broader QSR / industry in Canada
International business comparable sales of 5% are outperforming McDonald’s
Business being simplified and returning to capital-light model
China business has returned to growth, and sale to new franchisee will / meaningfully accelerate growth
Carrols outperforming broader Burger King system, and refranchising to smaller / operators proceeding ahead of schedule
Consistent 8% operating profit growth
On track to deliver 8% operating profit growth despite lower contribution from / unit growth
Remains cheap relative to intrinsic value and peers
Trades at 18x our estimate of next twelve months’ earnings, more than a 20% / discount to peers
Source: Company filings, PSCM estimates, Wall Street research. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.

**Visuals**

Line chart of QSR share price from Jun-12 to Feb-26. Y axis: Share price in dollars, ticks $10, $30, $50, $70, $90 (about $10 to $90), with a vertical axis line and tick marks. X axis ticks: Jun-12, Dec-13, Jun-15, Jan-17, Jul-18, Jan-20, Jul-21, Jan-23, Jul-24, Feb-26. One thick royal-blue line: starts about $15 in Jun-12, rises to about $27 by mid-2014, about $50 in early 2017, about $78 peak in late 2018/early 2019, a sharp drop to about $28 in early 2020, recovers to about $60-68 in 2021, dips to about $47 in late 2022, climbs to about $83 peak in 2024, and ends about $71 in Feb-26, labelled $71 in blue to the right of the line end. A grey dashed horizontal line at about $16 spans the plot width, with a grey-bordered light grey box labelled Average Cost at Announcement: $16*. Seven pale yellow callout boxes with grey borders have dotted grey leader lines pointing to the corresponding line points: Tim Hortons (~Aug 2014, $26), Popeyes (~Feb 2017, $50), Firehouse Subs (~Nov 2021, $57), Josh Kobza (~Feb 2023, $67), Carrols (~Jan 2024, $78), Patrick Doyle (~Nov 2022, $60, line descends from box top), China business sale (~Nov 2025, $70). No legend, no logos.

### Slide 69

**Text**

Howard Hughes Holdings (“HHH”)
2025 marked a pivotal year for HHH as it began its transformation into a / leading diversified holding company
Strong results across real estate subsidiary’s high-quality portfolio of MPCs
2025 MPC EBT guidance of $450 million reflects record-high land sale profitability
Same-store NOI growth driven by rental rate increases and strong leasing activity
$3.8 billion of contracted future revenue from condo towers in the pipeline
Well-positioned for substantial future free cash flow generation
Completed transaction with Pershing Square in May 2025
HHH began operating as a holding company seeking to acquire controlling interests in / high-quality durable growth companies
Pershing Square’s $900 million primary investment provided HHH with financial / flexibility to execute its strategic transformation
Signed definitive agreement to acquire Vantage in December 2025
Transaction is expected to close in Q2 2026, subject to regulatory approvals
We expect Vantage to materially accelerate HHH’s return and growth profile
Source: Company filings. This slide contains Pershing Square’s own views and opinions, based on publicly available information, to illustrate Pershing Square’s thinking on the matters therein.
69

**Layout**

Title at top left above a full-width navy rule. Below, a narrow left column holds the Howard Hughes logo, separated by a thin vertical black line from a right column of three stacked coloured header bars, each followed by bullets. Source footnote at bottom left, page number at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Howard Hughes script logo (black handwritten 'Howard Hughes' with a thin blue underline) at upper left. Thick navy horizontal rule under the title. Thin vertical black line separating the logo column from the content. Three coloured header bars (green, blue, maroon) plus an indigo banner at top. Green check marks beside the first four bullets; blue right-pointing triangle bullets beside the remaining four.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background. Title in bold blue #3A75C4 Arial Black, about 4.4% of slide height. Banner bars use white bold Arial: indigo #333399, green #509467, blue #4D83CB, maroon #800000. Body text is black bold Arial at about 2.4% of height. Check marks are green #509467 and triangle bullets blue #3A75C4. Footnote is small italic Arial. Flat style with square corners, no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 70

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: the density and the tiny footnotes, the heavy text-only bullet pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17), and the overlapping callouts and crowded axes on the share price charts (slides 55, 57, 70) mean this works as a read-ahead document more than a presented deck.
- Suggested repair: Cut or reduce the text-only strategic update pages (slides 7, 13, 15, 17) to a single visual or a short summary, and thin the callouts on the crowded price charts such as slides 55, 57 and 70.
